My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XX/M/M_00077/index.shtml -- 77/M]] What measures will you take to ensure that the years of efforts of the Theresian Military Academy can finally be successfully completed in recognition of the high school status?
ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00052_d7e3670	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00052_d7e3686	This is a very important matter for the Austrian federal army. I have already given the task of putting officer training on new foundations a year after I took office, namely in December 1991, in that there should not only be a higher share of educational elements, but in particular a greater use-oriented training should take place, and that it should also be internationally compatible, i.e. that it should be geared towards higher education in value. There have then been appropriate considerations, and since 1992 the project has been pursued to give the military academy also technical high school status. As a technical college itself, it cannot be recognized according to our legal situation, but as a high-school course, because for a vocational college at least two courses and a minimum student number of 1,000 would be required, which is not given at the military academy. The preparatory work on this was started and carried out in so far as we have reworked the complete officer's degree, so that it meets all the requirements of a university degree. At the same time, we have had it evaluated by the Swiss University of Technology in Zurich and there we have also achieved the best evaluation for the training programme, which is possible at all. That is why in 1995 we submitted this request to the College of Applied Sciences Council, and one year later, in June of this year, we made an improved request, based on suggestions from the Council of Applied Sciences. This request was also on the agenda of the last University Council, but has not been dealt with due to the abundance of applications.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00052_d7e5335	Mr. Federal Minister! My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XX/M/M_00074/index.shtml -- 74/M]] How do you view the security situation in Europe today?
ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00052_d7e5363	Mr. Federal Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00052_d7e5378	Ladies and gentlemen! After the end of the cold war, we remain in the largest process of transformation, which has been recorded in Europe's security policy over the last few centuries, and are still facing a great deal of impact, in the sense of a greater likelihood of regional and local conflicts in the absence of a global threat. At the same time, we see that there are intensive stabilisation efforts for Europe, which are highly promising, because one must assume that the only way to prevent wars in the future is to stabilise by programme. If one assumes what theoretical possibilities there are, then in my view, there are a total of three: One is the stabilisation by the domination of one or more powers – that is something no one wants – the second would theoretically be a balance of forces – which is in fact an impossibility because the potentials in the individual countries are so different that there is no lasting balance of forces – but de facto remains only one real possibility to prevent wars in Europe in the future, and that is integration. And the organisation, which has the highest potential for integration, is undoubtedly the EU at political level, which is undoubtedly NATO at the security level and the Western European Union in connection with these two. [[Congratulations on MEPs of the ÖVP.]] In all three organisations, there is strong effort to extend this integration to the whole of Europe as well.

On the one hand, a partnership with Russia is sought, and on the other hand, the integration of new states into NATO, which will ultimately make it possible to integrate all of Europe in such a way that in the future wars throughout Europe are as impossible as they are now between Germany and France.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00052_d7e6175	The next speaker is Mrs Tichy-Schreder. – Voluntary speaking time: 10 minutes.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00052_d7e6197	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Opening-time debates here in Parliament are always accompanied by strong media publicity, as retail firms are as interested as retail employees and, of course, customers and consumers. Five and a half years ago, the tradesmen felt threatened by liberalisation – this year the dealers were. Critic consumers again cannot understand a restriction on opening hours at all, as the Liberal Forum's request shows, since there are longer opening hours in countries around Austria. – In all arguments, there is a grain of truth, but just a grain of truth. To better understand the concerns and needs of those concerned, I have tried to research some basic data. In order to make this knowledge available to you as well, I will distribute relevant documents and material to you following my speech. It is important that you, too, know what it is about in this sector. The trading companies complained about the longer opening hours now fixed – we will now decide to extend from 60 to 66 hours – and their argument is that: Longer opening hours do not mean sales increases, but higher costs. Ladies and gentlemen! This argument is based on numbers. The sales per sales area was 52 500 S in 1983/84, and in 1993/94 the sales per sales area was 48 300 S. This means a minus of 4 200 S within ten years. The total cost in 1983/84 was 28.4 percent for a trading margin of 29 percent. In 1993/94 the total cost was 33.5 percent and the trading margin was 32.2 percent. The operating result was from a slight increase of 0.6 percent to a decrease of 1.3 percent – and that within ten years. In terms of content, however, I would point out that the revision of the Labour Peace Act represents the exact opposite of flexibilization in the field of working time and, in connection with the opening of the shop, brings only another regulation to the detriment of small trading companies. Ladies and gentlemen! It is clear that, due to the urge to release every second Saturday when working after 1 p.m. on Saturday, half the staff can only be provided with half the service. I believe that this is not a consumer-friendly attitude – not at a time of service that has already been discussed so much today. It is also clear that liberalisation cannot be achieved through inclusion in working time issues. It seems to me that it cannot, at the end of the day, be the same as the beginning of liberalisation. Liberalization, yes, but additional steps must be taken to ensure that the small and medium-sized enterprises are maintained in the interests of local services. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Perhaps, as parliamentarians, we also need to take clear steps in the spatial planning, which is, in principle, state-owned, in order to limit the unabated growth of the giants on the outskirts of the city and to secure local supply in the countryside. The same is true when it comes to not depopulation the city’s cores and not being destroyed. [[New congratulations to the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! In this context, please consider how very limited regulations actually make new ones difficult in the field of flexibilization! If I remember that within normal working time, in trade, supplements are to be granted, it simply does not fit into a liberal system, so it simply does not fit into our classification which, I believe, we have to represent in the future. This belongs to thought! That's against any flexibilization. And if collective treaty provisions – and I say so very consciously here – cannot be regulated at a collective contractual level, then we must consider whether we as parliamentarians are not invited to take action here as well. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] I've already said it: If the framework conditions are right, you can fully liberalize. Liberalization must not, however, be interpreted in such a way as to tear down all the barriers to the opening hours. A complete release of shop opening times would only bring in unsafe and many cheaper jobs. At the end of the day, no one could afford to this extent to the existing competitive staff. We do not need poorly paid or unsafe jobs or endangered companies, but we need more work on the right conditions to ensure that there are safe jobs in healthy businesses. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The new opening law is right and good. It's going in the right direction. We should see the whole problem in the same way, positive, positive, and achieve positive results in all areas – in the interests of entrepreneurs, workers and consumers!

ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00052_d7e7066	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! In recent weeks, the headlines of the media have been dominated by "plenty tuition for the new opening law" and similar sentences. "Trade in change" – under this motto, a business council conference of trade workers of the trade union of private employees in Upper Austria was held more than a decade ago. The main issue at that time was the introduction of goods management systems and the increasing flexibility of working hours. At the time, the main concerns were the loss of quality of the profession by the removal of work contents and the use of working time organisations as a rationalisation instrument. The assessment at that time was unfortunately confirmed by the actual development. In Austria, the trading landscape is now dominated by only a few companies. In addition, these dominant companies have to understand only more Group decisions, which are often taken abroad, because of their links with capital. The pressure for expansion of large companies is not only based on the price, but also, as we now see, on opening-up policy. As far as the latter is concerned, economic arguments such as the buy-in power flow and the needs of consumers have repeatedly been taken into account as a reason for a change in shop opening times. In fact, liberalisation can be seen at the opening hours as an instrument of displacement competition, but also as a concession of changed social structures. This development, ladies and gentlemen, is first of all the employees in the retail sector and their families. This concern will not, I believe, be limited to retail workers. There's hardly any more of them, these classic salesmen. They may still exist in the typical retail trade, in the up-to-date textile trade and the like. Today, the supply trade is only taking place on a self-service basis, just as there are different specialist markets for perfumery products, shoes et cetera. In recent years, a large number of activities have been successful in overcoming consumers. Just think of the self-service with cart, with deposit and so on. It is characteristic of the skills of employees in the so-called supply trade that practically no more commercial training is required. Relaying only means that the goods are presented in a manner previously defined by the sales management and then continuously supplemented. Less and less retail firms recruit their employees from their own apprenticeship training. The number of apprentices in the tradesmen's profession has fallen markedly. This also means that there are no professional prospects here. The greatest burden on trade workers is the increasingly unattractive working time. There are also several reasons for this. Working time and opening time are largely decoupled. In retail trade, there is a particularly high proportion of part-time workers, almost exclusively women. There are very many single parents among them. The proportion of divorces is above average. This results in a particularly strong economic dependency ratio, which is often applied carelessly by employers as a means of printing. I therefore conclude: The problems of employees in the context of the flexibility of working time in trade are further aggravated by the expansion of shop opening times. The Institute for Social Sciences and Economics in Linz has also been involved in a study on the problem of trade workers. The results showed exactly those problems that are also being discussed today. My conclusions are therefore: Despite the conditions imposed by the opening hours under competitive pressure, it is possible to find acceptable solutions for employees in trade, which, in particular, depends on the following framework conditions: if longer-term planning is possible between working time and leisure time; if there is a good climate in the business or in the department between superiors and employees; if to some extent it is possible to shape working time according to their own needs; if, ultimately, the operation also takes into account the conditions of living of the workers in the agreement of working hours. The practice will be shown by the decision in the Labour Peace Act, according to which every second Saturday is to be released as compensation for work on Saturday after 1 p.m., whether it is actually implemented by the companies. The competent authorities are invited to ensure compliance with monitoring. The announcement of the reception of additional employees will also be taken into account, as the practice previously followed with extended opening hours clearly showed that in most cases only a dilution of the occupation occurred without actual increase of staff. Ladies and gentlemen! The practice will show what the "Walk in commerce" actually brings.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00052_d7e7782	The next speaker is Mrs Mag. Dr. Fekter. Please, honourable Member, you are speaking.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00052_d7e7803	Mr President. Mr. Minister! High house! I allow myself to say a few things to the commercial law novel, which really does not deserve this name – this name is unfortunately chosen – because the core part of this commercial law novel 1996 is the creation of the commercial register. In other words, there will be a central commercial register in Austria, located at the Ministry of Economic Affairs, and a decentralized commercial register with the main district teams. This is gratifying to the extent that the district headquarters in central government have agreed on a uniform procedure in the collection of data on commercial rights. They are interconnected, and the central commercial register is also networked with the federal police. That is, the Federal Police can include in their investigations commercial rights in the future. It is a public file that can also be used in the modern investigation methods in origin. I welcome the fact that, despite initial difficulties, the federal states are taking a lead, and I congratulate the Ministry of Economic Affairs for having done so in long negotiations. In principle, the commercial register is public, and information may be provided to everyone. Insolvency notes and reasons for withdrawal are excluded; they must not be passed on to third parties. In addition to the commercial register, however, the new commercial law in 1996 also includes a substantial adjustment to the WTO agreement. In other words, authorised foreigners may also exercise their trade at home, under the same conditions as nationals, provided that they are not bound or subject to authorisation. If the word has already been spoken by committed or authorised trades, let me say a few words about the future business regulation. I am one of those who are in favour of liberalisation, in order to ensure that it is possible to work beyond the current relatively narrow framework of competence in neighbouring countries. I am in favour of a decisive extension of these neighbouring rights. I am also in favour of creating partial rights, namely, where someone does not need the full extent of a master's exam for his own activity. I am in favour of gathering very close trade and streamlining the trade list as a whole. However, I have great concerns, ladies and gentlemen, that under the word 'liberalisation of the Industrial Code' we are strongly weakening one of the pillars of apprenticeship training. Let me give you an example: When an Artist Silk painting works and sews blouses from her silk-painted cloths, she currently needs a tailor's championship. I am in favour of the fact that if it finds customers for their works of art or for their textiles, it may both sew and sell and be able to move independently on the market without having to take the master's examination for the tailor's craft. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] If we create such looser access, however, we must not allow those who have never learned the craftsmanship to take on apprenticeship training for this craft. Because someone who has never learned the tailor's craft can of course not train tailor's apprentices. – So far, so good, many would agree with me here. Only the effect – in the medium term – will be that in ten years we will probably only have more than 20 percent master businesses and 80 percent other, more free access. And who, ladies and gentlemen, are our apprentices? – That is to say, seeing apprenticeships disconnected from a liberal debate, I think grossly negligent. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] If we take apprenticeship seriously, we must ensure that we do not create framework conditions that weaken our excellent dual system. And the solution, ladies and gentlemen, cannot be that, instead of training in businesses, the young people are all sent to central teaching workshops or schools. That's not a right approach. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] In other words, it is a particular concern to be involved in the liberalisation of the trade regulations to which I recognize myself, and to point out that we should maintain our apprenticeship training system in good quality, as is the case at present.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00052_d7e8973	All three amendments which Mrs Tichy-Schreder has read to the full bear the necessary number of signatures and are included in the proceedings. Mr Wallner has spoken. – Ladies and gentlemen, you are speaking. Time limit: 4 minutes.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00052_d7e8997	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The economic governance laws are on our agenda today. One could ask the question of the meaning of these economic governance laws. The meaning of all these laws is to be prepared for the emergency case. Now you could ask yourself: What crisis could happen in Austria, which has been a member of the European Union for some time? . I must say that there are some examples in the recent past, too, of how important these economic governance laws can be in the case of crisis. Let us think, for example, of the Chernobyl reactor accident in 1986! There are also nuclear power plants on Austria's borders, Slovenia, Slovakia and the Czech Republic. If there were to be a similar reactor accident, then the fatal consequences would have for Austrian agriculture and also for European agriculture. In such cases, an economic governance law, namely the Food Management Act, is needed. Or: We think of the oil price shock of the 1970s or the crisis in the neighbouring former Yugoslavia, where it also looked a few days after the outbreak of the war, as if this conflict could go beyond Austrian territory. What economic governance laws are there? – There is the Oil Supply and Reporting Act, the Energy Steering Act, the Supply Security Act and the Food Management Act. Short to the 1982 oil supply law. This is an implementing law of the IEP Convention which requires Austria to lay down oil reserves to the extent that an outcome of at least 90 days without oil imports is guaranteed. To this end, all importers of petroleum and petroleum products are required to maintain reserves of 25 percent of the previous year's imports. In practice, it has been seen that this stockpiles of course lead to high costs for importers. Many importers have therefore tried to circumvent these provisions. This novel provides for a repair of these loopholes. Of course, it is cost-effective to have to store such large reserves. Let's think about the storage costs. In winter, for example, the oil must be heated, it must be kept in motion and moved around constantly. In this case, a revision is necessary in order to avoid circumvention. Just a short while to the Supply Security Act: It is interesting which areas would be affected in this crisis case: tobacco, mineral substances, chemical products, plastics, skins and skins of any kind. To the Energy Steering Act: Of course, this affects oil, gaseous and solid fuels and electricity. To the Food Management Act: These include, above all, food, but also drinking water, animals that can be processed into food, fertilisers, plant protection products and much more. I believe that these economic governance laws are very important for our country, so it is essential to extend and modernise. I wish all of us and the people that if we need these laws, they will never come into force! Thank you.

When we reaffirm our energy policy objectives, adopted three years ago by the Federal Government, recently reaffirmed in the energy report, once again, which are: high security of supply, high degree of economic efficiency, social compatibility and also environmental compatibility, and which confronts the real situation in Austria, then we can see overall – and I will come to the details – a very positive balance of the situation, especially this target is comparison. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] On the topic of security of supply: I believe that in hardly any other country, the level of security of supply, namely self-sufficiency, is as high as with us. We are highly self-evident, so this objective can be considered to be achieved and fulfilled. With regard to economics, some critical words are quite appropriate, considering individual companies. But the development in the individual companies that have been set up there and the result is quite positive. It is precisely the last Court of Auditors report on the composite society, in which there is still a lot of criticism and improvement in terms of economic efficiency, efficiency, that has confirmed that the path that is going to take, including other ETUCs, is quite the right one. On social tolerance, I believe, it should be noted that this is not only to be seen in the final consumer price. It is a mix of security of supply and quality, as well as price formation and – not to mention – environmental compatibility. I believe that the popular vote on Zwentendorf has shown that the population – is consumers alike – sees social tolerance not only at the lowest price that we all want, of course, although at the time one has also tried to sell this project to the population from the point of view of price reduction. Very briefly on the subject of environmental compatibility. We are confirmed worldwide that we have a very low CO2 emissions per capita, especially in the field of energy generation, clearly below the EU average, that we have a very favourable relationship between total energy consumption and CO2 emissions throughout the OECD comparison. For example, the International Energy Agency confirms that we have a high level of efficiency in energy use. This is the national environmental plan which was adopted by the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection on 14 November. It was presented by Mr. Stefan Schleicher in November 1996. And this National Environmental Plan has a single crucial and central statement, which reads as follows: Austria is able to reach the Toronto target by 2005. – A higher praise from the experts cannot get Austria. There are also the conditions in which this can happen. This is possible if primary energy use is reduced by 11 percent compared to 1990. I should like to point out: Austria is in an excellent position with this data. The United States of America with its oil consumption of 20 litres per day and per capita will never achieve this high-level Toronto target. Of course, nothing happens by itself in energy policy. There is a need for massive effort. These measures are also included in the National Environmental Plan, and I should like to mention them here: Firstly, it is necessary to increase the efficiency of energy use as a whole. Currently, the efficiency is on average 43 percent, which is relatively low. We could only halve primary energy use in the long term. Secondly, Priority for the use of renewable energy sources, such as biomass, wind and sun. Thirdly, forced use of solar energy. It is gratifying that, by spring 1996, 183 kW of installed power was already provided by the Republic of Austria. What I think is particularly crucial is energy management in all the cities and communities of Austria. We must help each municipality to submit local or regional energy plans. Especially in a country where the wood grows practically to the window, it is not useful for the heat supply of small units of wood, oil or gas from over 5,000 kilometres away. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] What is still necessary are energy saving measures in the field of construction. Here the public hand has an exemplary effect, it must have it. I would like to see systematic measures to improve the heating of public buildings in Austria. The last point is the use of cascadean energy, especially of heat, for example in hospitals. Finally, Austria should also benefit from the forced development of all technologies that implement the principle of sustainability in the energy industry. Overall, these measures can only be implemented in close cooperation with the existing energy supply companies, and that is why the Austrian People's Party is committed to extending the General Agreement, and that is why we will also accept this request with conviction.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00052_d7e12022	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! High house! The economic miracle of Austria is truly a proverbial one. Until the early 1990s, we had achieved great economic data in this country. These figures were due, not least, to the development of tourism. By 1992 Austria had taken over the 160 billion schilling of foreign exchange from tourism and thus almost, of course, covered the chronic trade deficit. It has become quite natural for our country that we can afford to import 70, 80, yes 100 billion schillings more than to export, because the guests come anyway and bring the necessary foreign exchange to compensate for this false amount. In 1992 we went through a break in tourism development. What happened? – The long-term causes can be found in a modest cost increase that has fully hit prices in a service-intensive sector in the field of personal service. This development continues. The cost of bureaucracy, labour costs, additional labour costs, disposal costs and so on, and so on, have increased steadily, in part, step by step, so that we have lost market shares in global competition. There is no industry in Austria, which is as dependent on the global displacement competition as tourism. There is no such open market in which tourism has had to prove itself for many decades. The decline in air prices and the break in monetary relations in 1992 have done a lot more. The effect is the numbers we stand for today. Nocturnal figures should not be given too much importance. It's just a count of nights. Nevertheless, it is impressive that these fell from 130 million to 112 million in 1996. Much more serious for our country is the decline in foreign exchange revenue from 160 to 140 billion schilling annually, which has led to the fact that in 1996 the share of tourism revenue in gross domestic product will not be 8.4 percent more than in 1992 but only more than 6%. That is, Austria has lost a 2 percent chance of growth. This is a GDP chance. At the same time, growth in gross domestic product in Austria has also declined. This personal consumption through tourism has failed, and in 1996, Austria – and unfortunately this is forecast for 1997 too – has the lowest growth rates in the European Union as a whole. In addition, the current account deficit has been out of control. It rose from 1.6 billion schilling in 1992 to 50 billion schilling. This is 2% of gross domestic product in 1996. And that's the second year in a row! The absence of tourism revenue and the rise in tourism expenditure – both effects are included here – have already exhausted some of the foreign exchange reserves, and the Austrian National Bank is now pursuing a policy with closed eyes towards the euro, according to the motto: We will do it by 1998! Surprisingly, the decline in employment has also remained without commenting on policy. Instead of 161 000 employees in 1992, there were only 155,000 in 1996. Ladies and gentlemen! Not only has this been done in the national accounts, which I have already explained, but, of course, it has also left deep traces in the farms. For example, the annual burden on the four- and five-star hotels, the parade companies of our country, fell from 44 to 41 percent. The effect is painful. The impact is threatening for the whole country. Nevertheless, the Federal Government has so far kept silent or meant to be able to speak up. One has long argued and said that this is such a small economic break that it will be again. No one wanted to believe what is painfully certain today, namely that in 1992 we went through a line of development which will continue until the turn of the millennium. I predict for the turn of the millennium about 100 million nights in Austria. To put the sizes in the right light: Even then Austria remains the most tourism-intensive country in Europe. Even then, Vorarlberg and Tirol together still have more respites than Greece. Despite this development, with all the painful consequences for the national accounts and also for the establishments that I have already explained, we remain in the tourist sector. The second effect in the public sector was a criticism of the willingness to provide services in the industry. Both statements had their effects. Although the Federal Government, which obviously did not take this issue seriously, believed that it was only a short-term phenomenon, the framework conditions in the service sector have worsened costably since then. I'm just briefly counting what happened in the last one, two years: Energy tax, the new calculation of holiday compensation and 13th and 14ths. Social security income, exorbitantly high water and garbage charges, high red tape costs through the Workers' Protection Act, increase in social security contributions and the maximum basis of assessment, increase the insolvency compensation fund from 0.1 to 0.7 percent and so on and so on. All this interests only insofar as the question is to ask: What cost increases have the government parties of this House decided here are the international guests and also the Austrian guests willing to pay? That's the question. You can make laws here, as many as you want. The decision whether the costs you incur are paid by the market, whether by the Austrian guest or by the international guest, will be in retrospect, and it will be to the detriment of Austria. In the global displacement competition, we have not been able to implement the costs on the market, because our customers make the price. Our customers say what the performance is worth. They decide whether to purchase or not to purchase performance at this or that price, at this or that quality level. Quality is what the guest pays. Exploitation times price is the game. Exploitation has decreased, the price also, which in all categories of operations, including the top hotel industry, has led to high yield losses. A third of the industry today faces the financial ruin. The second criticism that was made, that of the performance, was very positive. It has led the industry to get better. Their criticism, ladies and gentlemen, the criticism of Austrian guests, the criticism of the media has led companies to learn that they simply need to improve their performance in order to satisfy the high-strengthed requirements of a critical guest. More customer orientation has learned the successful third of top companies. To achieve better performance has learned the second third of the companies fighting for their existence. Living in smaller sales expectations, all businesses have had to experience painfully. It was not to be believed that this growth would continue indefinitely over 40 years. It was to be expected that a consolidation phase would occur. This consolidation phase occurred after the 1992 break-up and became an open crisis in the summer of 1996. Cooperation in tourism will be the command of the hour in the future, because not the sum of the individual offers is sold in the valleys, in the towns, in the cities, but the product made out of it. The word "destination management" summarizes this. It only needs to be understood throughout the country of understanding that tourism is a sui generis industry, first because it works in a completely open market in global competition, second because only 50 percent of the purchasing power of the guest's holiday budget goes directly to the tourism companies – hospitality and gastronomy – and the second half flows directly into all other sectors and therefore tourism has a very, very high multiplier effect on employment. There is no industry that has such a high regional policy component. There are entire valleys, whole lake shores, entire areas in Austria, which would be potential settlement areas without the added value of tourism. The guest, who flies from Austria to Tunisia, for example, undergoes a metamorphosis. He will be a wonderful, great luxury butterfly because he will rise in a country with a soft currency and low wages and will be able to provide the services of a five-star hotel that is forbidden in Austria. The same thing happens to the guest who drives from Tunisia to us. Even if he has a relatively high income there, it will be difficult for him to maintain his standard of living in our hard currency and high cost levels. This is the reason why the cheap flight prices usually work – not only but mostly – only in one direction stimulatingly. Ladies and gentlemen! Nevertheless, Austria is full of market opportunities. I believe in this product! This country has excellent infrastructural capacity for leisure and tourism. There are so beautiful areas in Austria that cannot be thought away from the world's tourist atlas. We're in the middle of the markets. With our Central and South Eastern European neighbours, new markets are growing. As a cultural destination, we are at the top of Europe. We still have a functioning superstructure in the field of businesses, although a third is already threatening to break away and break away, and we have a fantastic infrastructure. Ladies and gentlemen! To sum up, Austria has without a doubt the best tourist product in the world from Lake Neusiedler to Lake Constance. So why can't you take advantage of these fantastic market opportunities? – There are many own mistakes, many of the industry’s own weaknesses that cannot be discussed here, which the industry itself has to deal with. But there is also, above all, the scope of the framework conditions which we have to discuss here in Parliament, which are relevant at local level, at country level and, above all, at federal level. Tourism is now always going from hard-currency countries to soft-currency countries. People want to be paid at home in hard currency and spend their money where their currency is worth a lot. Think of the internal value of the American dollar, which is at 16 or 17 S respectively, and you can buy it at 11 S on the switch. Tourism is always going from high wage countries to low wage countries. People want to earn a lot at home and, of course, where they go on vacation, are served by people who earn little. They don't say that implicitly, but that's their travel decision if they choose cheap destinations. Turning this way around is our task. This will be very difficult. But one thing is clear: We will never be able to win this competition through the prize at the cost location Austria. We may be able to gain it over quality, with quality having nothing to do with stars, but with the type of service requested by the customer that meets its needs. Minister of Tourism! The demands for the Federal Government go there to really address this issue. Since 1992 and ever more intensive since 1993 and 1994, we have been talking about this issue, we are drawing attention to the fact that there is a real problem in this country. And nothing has happened! The economic data, if already the individual data are not interested, are disastrous. I would prefer to pursue a policy in which funding is used to ensure that enough foreigners come to finance the beds. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] The focus will be clearly placed on innovation, cooperation, acquisition or participation in modern booking networks, up to some kind of incentive system also for domestic travel agencies to transfer domestic holidaymakers with greater pleasure and financial satisfaction instead of to the Canary Islands. As far as funding policy is concerned, whatever will trigger disput, I have to go from traditional interest-rate subsidies policy to a policy of interest-rate caps. Only if interest rates exceed a certain level should be increased to offset risk, but not a priority at a time with enormously low interest rates should be given additional interest subsidies. An open word on the question of new building capacities. At the moment I have more interventions for the new building than for the closure of hotels on my table – for the latter I do not have at all. Therefore, there should be no disagreement in this regard: As already announced, the Ministry of Economic Affairs has commissioned a study in which we will test the destabilization of Austrian entrepreneurs of the hotel industry and gastronomy. We expect the results by the end of January and mid February respectively. Then it will be time to have a discussion with all the responsibilities, especially with the federal states, both as a regional regulator and as housing financiers, in order to negotiate exit models reasonably. I am here very much with Mr Peter, I would like to expressly draw this out on one example: It can't be that wherever we want to stop a hotel, you're immediately afraid that it's going to be a sale to strangers or a vacation home that stops the whole thing again. – This once for financing in general. Question 3: We are currently examining all the complaints in detail, which are based on the fact that it will not take long until visas are issued. From South Africa there are over 35 000 particularly well-paid winter guests, who do not complain about the duration of the visa, but about the fact that they need a visa at all. This is one thing that we must also agree with the EU, which is no longer just our own competence. The same applies to visas for Singapore or Taiwan. We have had some problems with the rejection of guests at the borders. There will also be a need for discussion with customs frontiers. We'll check every case. Where visas are needed, we have ensured that it is done as soon as possible. To come to the next question: We will also have to ensure, under the terms of the Schengen Agreement, that an authorised official, who has access to the Schengen computer, guarantees the issue of visas in all foreign offices of Austria, whether that is a message, a general consulate or a trade delegation. Eastern Europe is one of our great hope markets. I know that just in the area from which I came - south of Lower Austria - this summer without the guests from Eastern Europe would have been disastrous. So a very good result was achieved. I think kindness would be very useful to these guests. I have also caused food cards to appear in the languages of our eastern neighbours as soon as possible. I was repeatedly told when visiting the neighbouring countries that it is incredible that every Austrian expects to find, for example, a German menu in Ödenburg, but is amazed when a Hungarian expects the same in Wiener Neustadt. That's just a marginal remark. On the question of external economic organisation. I believe that a critical examination is necessary, which outside bodies are required, where a merger is appropriate to have the synergies of a common roof, of common costs. The end of the discussion and the end of the examination has not yet been reached, but it is my firm will. The budgetary approaches to the Austrian advertising are set out for two years. We will have to discuss this again during the budgetary negotiations in the spring of next year. Especially in his term of office, much has happened in the field of compensation and reparation. I would like to mention a few measures: Conscious of Austria's moral responsibility for the crimes of National Socialism, the Republic of Austria has established a National Fund for the Victims of National Socialism on the occasion of the 50th anniversary of the Second Republic. As you have already mentioned, ladies and gentlemen, this fund was created at the National Council in 1995 and is used to provide benefits to the victims of National Socialism. By the end of this year, the Fund has paid a total of over 700 million Schilling to almost 10,000 applicants. 4000 applicants were from the United States, more than 1 000 from Israel and nearly 2 000 from Austria. Over the next two years, the fund will be disbursed with a further 600 million schilling each. Individual payments are expected to be completed by 1998, according to the National Fund Communication. Material benefits are, of course, only a limited part of the reparation, but I believe it should be a sign of the admonition and consciousness and reconciliation, a sign that the younger generation has not forgotten the injustice and the madness that happened at that time. Another example I would like to cite is the Berlin-Beneficiction, which took place in Vienna at the end of October of this year. It recalls the tragic chapter in Austrian history, but at the same time also represents an important and extraordinary legacy for the victims and for the survivors of the Holocaust. The result of this auction is intended for those in need who have been persecuted by the Nazi regime for religious, racial or political reasons and whose descendants. This auction brought a total proceeds of over 155 million schilling. In my opinion, the President of the Israeli Cultural Community, Paul Grosz, rightly spoke of a phenomenal result for the compensation of the victims of genocide. However, I would like to show you some other measures that have been taken to support the victims of the Holocaust: for example, the Code of Victims for Care. For pensions, health care, orthopaedic care and other expenses, nearly 8 billion schilling have been spent since 1945 until the end of this year. This year alone, a budget of 261 million schillings is planned. 258 million schilling was paid out from the aid fund for reparation payments to NS victims who have their residence and permanent residence abroad between 1988 and 1995. In the period 1963-1995, 204 million Schillings were allocated to grants, 19 million Schillings to subsidies and 231 million Schillings to loans. The Social Law Amending Act was amended in 1993 and states that persons who were in the sixth year of life in March 1938 are also benefiting from the post-purchasing scheme. There is a visit programme, the elderly Austrians, who had to leave their country under the Nazi rule and now want to make a visit to Austria again, provide financial support. The Israeli Cultural Community was supported in 1993 with around 17.7 million schilling for the integration of Jewish immigrants in Austria. Since the Civil Service Act Novelle 1991, there has been the possibility that the activities of civil servants at Holocaust Memorials abroad will also be counted as civil service. At present, this possibility is perceived by the association for the performance of a memorial service at Holocaust Memorials. In addition, there are many other measures that I do not wish to take here. Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen, Stoisits! I believe that the measures taken are important, good and welcome. The measures have been taken with the aim of reconciling a part, a small part of the damage that has been done. This does not mean, however, that what has happened so far can be a conclusion among the aid measures. The aid must not come to a standstill. We will therefore continue to work fully to support these people. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] A lot has been done. – Even more to do is our common mission and our commitment, and I invite you, on behalf of the Federal Chancellor and on my own behalf, to sit down and try to explore and explore ways together for further support.

The list includes, as you know, 127 countries. Refugees come into a twinkling mill with a very strict interpretation of this provision. You need legal papers, that's clear, but refugees often travel illegally. If there is no conflict between Articles 28 to 38 and Article 30e, I am very satisfied with that. In any case, access to the asylum procedure is always guaranteed. However, some regulations are peculiar. How should carriers check what their clients or passengers are planning? Escape can also be necessary suddenly, without planning it; instead, heads d’etat have been found more often. If these companies were to sign a note that their clients or passengers would not apply for illegal asylum, that would be just ridiculous and would not be sufficient protection. What about data protection? The deletion of data – which is no longer necessary as provided for in Article 112 – is actually a very blurred matter. This point should still be clarified. It is also necessary to clarify the provision in Article 109, which concerns the right of information, which is equally vaguely formulated. I come to the end because it's late in the evening. The Nordic passport union – and I address this to my colleagues from the Freedom Party – has been in place since 1954. There has been neither a greater crime has been observed nor an overwhelming number of illegals nor an overwhelming number of asylum seekers. I therefore believe that we can have great confidence in the idea of Schengen. In my opinion, border security should be carried out by a European police force. The external borders should be monitored jointly as a result of the load balance. Now a group of 5,300 men is being set up to take over these tasks by the year 2000 or 2005 – you don't know exactly yet – and then these people lose their function. Where to go? Then what are they supposed to do? Or is this a calculated unemployment you are buying? But I still want to say: The free movement of persons is such a great value that I would like to support this idea of Schengen.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00052_d7e21311	Mr President. High house! Three reasons are from the point of view of the Austrian People's Party, why the Citizenship Act needs to be amended or amended. Point one: adaptation to international standards and standards. Point two: better integration of citizens. Finally, point three is the demand or the desire of the federal states for greater legal certainty and clarity of decision. Point one: international standards and standards. I have here a report of the daily newspaper "Die Presse" of 9. April, which reports on the forms in the Netherlands. That's what it says: "Social science and language courses are intended to facilitate integration into the Dutch society." And on top of that: "This will change in the future, at least for new arrivals who do not come from countries of the European Union and want to settle in the Netherlands. You, the non-EU citizens who want to receive social assistance or unemployment assistance from the Dutch State after their arrival, must have been in the first place. There is a so-called integration course in January. That's a duty. Those who still refuse to learn Dutch risk losing their state support funds. The citizenship course consists of two parts, a language course as well as the field of national and social science, where foreigners are informed about customs and customs in Holland and receive useful tips for visiting the town hall, the library and so on." The free Netherlands, so free Holland, ladies and gentlemen, where even Euthanasia is allowed, which I generally and strictly reject, a country in which drugs are partially released, has ordered refugees to learn the national language. This also takes into account the draft of our new law. Also in other countries, such as the USA, Canada, Hungary, Germany, Great Britain, Switzerland and so on, integration courses and knowledge of the national language are a prerequisite for citizenship. Point two: Improving the integration of citizens. I have a very special partner, namely the OSCE, and a report by Parliamentary Assembly in Stockholm from 5 to 9. July. It reports Polish Senator Jiai Zislak that the following factors hinder the integration of refugees and immigrants most: lack of or insufficient knowledge of the language of the country of residence, unemployment or inexperience in the search for work, establishment of immigrants in homogeneous settlements from which national enclaves arise in cities or communities. He also writes in his report: "Some states are not responding to the current situation. The opposition of the locals to immigrants and refugees often has the cause of differences in culture and religion, poor economic situation in the country of residence, too little contact with the native population of a country or a region with people of other races, nationality or religion." Finally, he concludes: "The knowledge of the language of the country in which the refugees or immigrants live is one of the prerequisites for integration in that country. This, in turn, is directly linked to the opportunities for better qualifications and a job." It is one, as I believe, very important interpretation of our request: If anyone wants to become a member of Austria if someone is looking for citizenship, it must also be necessary for him to be able to recognize our language in principle in our laws, just as it is standard in other countries. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] It should not play a role in the discussion as to whether integration courses should be paid by the public. That's, I think, by the way. But it is important that the one who wants to become an Austrian citizen is also the German language. The third point is the demand of the federal states for legal certainty and clarity of the law. It has composed a body of federal judges responsible for the exhibition of citizenship. We have presented a draft which is integrated in our request for the amendment of the Citizenship Act, which is intended to give legal certainty, which also calls for language courses for people wishing to be integrated, and which includes, above all, the fact that illegal residence in Austria is not to be considered as the basis for the period of residence on and on its own. These are essential and important points. I should like to ask you to discuss, in the sense of this proposal tabled by the Austrian People's Party, which is referred to the Committee on the Interior, so that we can reach a common decision, taking into account the modern standards that are common in Europe, in the world.

We have also been able to meet these interests through amendments. The objectives of this society are somewhat different from the former objectives of the Federal Forests, and they are – very ecologically oriented – to be managed in § 7: "1. the forest soil is sustainable; its productive power must be preserved and, as far as possible, improved; 2. the protection, welfare and recovery effects of the forest are best possible to safeguard and develop; 3. the drinking and utility water reserves must be preserved" – this was also a connection point for the §-27 application –; "4. the interests of agriculture, in particular of mountain farms, must be taken into account; 5. Land outside the forest, which is particularly suitable for recreational purposes, such as shores of the sea, can be made accessible, especially for those purposes; 6. for the design and conservation of nature parks and areas protected by nature conservation laws; 7. for wildlife management, care must be taken to ensure environmental balance; 8. rights must be guaranteed in accordance with the principle law on the treatment of forest and pasture use rights and special field services." These rights have thus been secured, also and especially in the interest of the federal state of Salzburg, where there is still an old dispute because of the Constitutional Law of 1 January 1989. In October, there was a transition to the federal constitution. § 11 reads: "All remaining state assets are assets of the federal government. The Minister has indicated that it is not the water law law novelle that we decide here today, but it is a necessary adjustment to the small-scale facilities. But we must also see the causes. We created the Water Law Act in 1990 and, because of the financial strength of the municipalities, we believed that we could quickly implement a sewerage programme. Today, 1996, we know that these funds are no longer possible. A municipality with 10 000 inhabitants has to bear costs of around 400 million schilling, which can only be financed by the fees. This means an increase of far more than 100 per cent in canal charges for the rural population, in some municipalities even per year. That is to say, the path between ecology and economics, which Mr Wabl has taken, is quite narrow, because the population must be able to do so, even if the Republic is contributing. This 1 billion schilling, which has also come out of the Water Management Fund for the Channel Development Fund, was certainly useful, but, to be said clearly, often also had to be used for pre-financing by municipalities and thus did not become directly active. Ladies and gentlemen! What do these micro-clearing systems mean? – A family with three people who do not have a channel connection does not live cheaper either. For if it has a dense chamber, it means a discharge of 7 cubic meters of dirty water in a fourteen-day rhythm. These 7 cubic meters of dirty water cost 1,500 s every 14 days, so the family costs 40,000 s annually. So what's the alternative? – The alternative is the famous overflow that we will have to deal with in the next few months and in the next few years, because the overflow is not good for the environment.
